A packet can therefore declare a\nfalsifying condition its oracle cannot detect, and nothing notices.\n\nA seed closes that. It is the reference overlay's complement: the reference\nmust turn the oracle GREEN, a seed must turn it RED. If a seeded defect leaves\nthe oracle green, the oracle does not test what the packet claims.\n\nThe subject is a new module, `templates/dispatch/scripts/defect_seeds.py`,\nexposing:\n\n    SEED_ID_RE\n        Compiled pattern for a seed id: starts with a letter, then letters,\n        digits, '.', '_' or '-'. The same shape as an acceptance property id,\n        because both name a requirement in evidence a human will read later.\n\n    parse_seeds(packet) -> list[dict]\n        Normalised `oracle.defect_seeds`, each {\"id\", \"patch\", \"expect_red\",\n        \"description\"}. A packet declaring none returns [] and is not an error.\n        Raises ValueError when a declaration is unusable.\n\n    seed_falsified(seed, results) -> bool\n        Whether this seed did its job: every command in `expect_red` ran and\n        every one of them was red.\n\n    unfalsified(packet, outcomes) -> list[str]\n        Seed ids that did not falsify, in packet order. `outcomes` maps seed id\n        to that seed's results. A seed with no outcome at all counts as\n        unfalsified -- silence is not evidence.\n\nRule 11: the subject is `defect_seeds.py`. No git, no subprocess, no file I/O.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport importlib.util\nimport sys\nimport unittest\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nsys.dont_write_bytecode = True\n\nROOT = Path(__file__).parents[3]\nSCRIPTS = ROOT / \"templates/dispatch/scripts\"\n\n\ndef _load_module(name: str, path: Path):\n    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(name, path)\n    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)\n    assert spec.loader is not None\n    spec.loader.exec_module(module)\n    return module\n\n\nseeds = _load_module(\"defect_seeds_subject\", SCRIPTS / \"defect_seeds.py\")\n\nGATE = \"agentops.dispatch.tests.example\"\nOTHER = \"agentops.dispatch.tests.other\"\n\n\ndef _packet(defect_seeds=None, starts_red=(GATE,)) -> dict:\n    oracle: dict = {\"starts_red\": list(starts_red)}\n    if defect_seeds is not None:\n        oracle[\"defect_seeds\"] = defect_seeds\n    return {\"oracle\": oracle}\n\n\ndef _seed(seed_id=\"empty-assertion\", patch=\"docs/evidence/seeds/a.patch\", expect_red=(GATE,), **kw):\n    seed = {\"id\": seed_id, \"patch\": patch, \"expect_red\": list(expect_red)}\n    seed.update(kw)\n    return seed\n\n\ndef _red(*ids):\n    return [{\"command_id\": i, \"exit_code\": 1} for i in ids]\n\n\ndef _green(*ids):\n    return [{\"command_id\": i, \"exit_code\": 0} for i in ids]\n\n\nclass ParseTests(unittest.TestCase):\n    def test_a_packet_with_no_seeds_is_not_an_error(self):\n        # Every packet frozen before this row has none, and must keep working.\n        self.assertEqual(seeds.parse_seeds(_packet()), [])\n\n    def test_a_packet_with_no_oracle_at_all_is_not_an_error(self):\n        self.assertEqual(seeds.parse_seeds({}), [])\n\n    def test_a_seed_is_normalised_with_a_description_default(self):\n        parsed = seeds.parse_seeds(_packet([_seed()]))\n        self.assertEqual(len(parsed), 1)\n        self.assertEqual(\n            set(parsed[0]), {\"id\", \"patch\", \"expect_red\", \"description\"}\n        )\n        self.assertEqual(parsed[0][\"description\"], \"\")\n\n    def test_a_declared_description_is_kept(self):\n        parsed = seeds.parse_seeds(_packet([_seed(description=\"list-only assertion\")]))\n        self.assertEqual(parsed[0][\"description\"], \"list-only assertion\")\n\n    def test_packet_order_is_preserved(self):\n        parsed = seeds.parse_seeds(\n            _packet([_seed(seed_id=\"b\"), _seed(seed_id=\"a\")])\n        )\n        self.assertEqual([s[\"id\"] for s in parsed], [\"b\", \"a\"])\n\n    def test_defect_seeds_must_be_a_list(self):\n        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):\n            seeds.parse_seeds(_packet({\"id\": \"a\"}))\n\n    def test_a_seed_must_declare_an_id(self):\n        seed = _seed()\n        del seed[\"id\"]\n        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):\n            seeds.parse_seeds(_packet([seed]))\n\n    def test_a_seed_id_must_match_the_id_pattern(self):\n        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):\n            seeds.parse_seeds(_packet([_seed(seed_id=\"1-leading-digit\")]))\n\n    def test_duplicate_seed_ids_are_refused(self):\n        # Two seeds under one id would make the evidence unreadable: a reader\n        # could not tell which defect the red belongs to.\n        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):\n            seeds.parse_seeds(_packet([_seed(seed_id=\"a\"), _seed(seed_id=\"a\")]))\n\n    def test_a_seed_must_declare_a_patch(self):\n        seed = _seed()\n        del seed[\"patch\"]\n        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):\n            seeds.parse_seeds(_packet([seed]))\n\n    def test_an_absolute_patch_path_is_refused(self):\n        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):\n            seeds.parse_seeds(_packet([_seed(patch=\"/etc/passwd.patch\")]))\n\n    def test_a_patch_path_escaping_the_repository_is_refused(self):\n        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):\n            seeds.parse_seeds(_packet([_seed(patch=\"../outside.patch\")]))\n\n    def test_expect_red_must_not_be_empty(self):\n        # A seed that expects nothing to go red proves nothing.\n        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):\n            seeds.parse_seeds(_packet([_seed(expect_red=[])]))\n\n    def test_expect_red_must_name_a_starts_red_command(self):\n        # Only the oracle's own commands are evidence here. A seed pointing at\n        # some other gate would be red for reasons the packet never froze.\n        with self.assertRaises(ValueError):\n            seeds.parse_seeds(_packet([_seed(expect_red=[OTHER])]))\n\n    def test_expect_red_may_name_several_starts_red_commands(self):\n        packet = _packet([_seed(expect_red=[GATE, OTHER])], starts_red=(GATE, OTHER))\n        self.assertEqual(seeds.parse_seeds(packet)[0][\"expect_red\"], [GATE, OTHER])\n\n    def test_the_packet_is_not_mutated(self):\n        seed = _seed()\n        packet = _packet([seed])\n        seeds.parse_seeds(packet)\n        self.assertEqual(packet[\"oracle\"][\"defect_seeds\"], [seed])\n        self.assertNotIn(\"description\", seed)\n\n\nclass FalsifiedTests(unittest.TestCase):\n    def test_a_seed_whose_expected_command_went_red_falsified(self):\n        self.assertTrue(seeds.seed_falsified(_seed(), _red(GATE)))\n\n    def test_a_seed_whose_expected_command_stayed_green_did_not(self):\n        # THE case. A seeded defect the oracle does not notice means the oracle\n        # does not test what the packet claims it tests.\n        self.assertFalse(seeds.seed_falsified(_seed(), _green(GATE)))\n\n    def test_a_seed_with_no_result_for_its_command_did_not_falsify(self):\n        # Silence is not evidence: a command that never ran has not shown\n        # anything, and must not be read as success.\n        self.assertFalse(seeds.seed_falsified(_seed(), []))\n\n    def test_every_expected_command_must_be_red_not_merely_one(self):\n        seed = _seed(expect_red=[GATE, OTHER])\n        self.assertFalse(seeds.seed_falsified(seed, _red(GATE) + _green(OTHER)))\n        self.assertTrue(seeds.seed_falsified(seed, _red(GATE, OTHER)))\n\n    def test_results_may_be_a_one_shot_iterator(self):\n        self.assertTrue(seeds.seed_falsified(_seed(), iter(_red(GATE))))\n\n\nclass UnfalsifiedTests(unittest.TestCase):\n    def test_a_packet_with_no_seeds_has_nothing_unfalsified(self):\n        self.assertEqual(seeds.unfalsified(_packet(), {}), [])\n\n    def test_a_seed_that_falsified_is_not_reported(self):\n        packet Structural check of a packet against the repository dispatch manifest.\n11: overlay    Emit the session-only OpenCode permission overlay for a packet.\n12: prepare    Create the disposable str,\n419: ) -> dict[str, Any]:\n420:     \"\"\"Read and validate the exact served reservation that authorizes this packet.\n421: \n422:     The worker never receives this authority.  This coordinator-side check\n423:     prevents a frozen packet from being dispatched after its authorization went\n424:     stale, moved to a different actor, or ceased to cover the intended item.\n425: \n426:     **Migrated from ``sprintctl claim`` (2026-08-23).**  That command no longer\n427:     exists: sprintctl removed credential-bearing claims in favour of advisory\n428:     reservations, so every packet on every host failed here regardless of its\n429:     contents.  The unit tests did not catch it because they mocked the old\n430:     payload shape, which is the failure mode of testing a CLI you never call.\n431: \n432:     The two are not a rename.  A claim was a lease with an expiry; a reservation\n433:     is explicitly \"a coordination signal, not a lease\" and carries no expiry at\n434:     all.  The expiry check is therefore replaced by a **staleness** check on\n435:     ``last_activity_at``, matching sprintctl's own 4-hour horizon: an\n436:     authorization nobody has touched in that long is not evidence that anyone is\n437:     still coordinating this work, and ``sprintctl reservation touch`` refreshes\n438:     it deliberately rather than by accident.  ``sprint_item.claim_id`` and\n439:     ``claim_actor`` keep their names so frozen packets stay readable; they now\n440:     hold the reservation id and its actor.\n441:     \"\"\"\n442:     item_ref = packet[\"sprint_item\"][\"ref\"]\n443:     _, _, item_text = item_ref.partition(\"#\")\n444:     item_id = int(item_text)\n445:     expected_claim_id = packet[\"sprint_item\"][\"claim_id\"]\n446:     expected_actor = packet[\"sprint_item\"][\"claim_actor\"]\n447:     try:\n448:         completed = subprocess.run(\n449:             [sprintctl_bin, \"reservation\", \"list\", \"--item-id\", str(item_id),\n450:              \"--active-only\", \"--json\"],\n451:             cwd=repo_root,\n452:             text=True,\n453:             capture_output=True,\n454:             check=False,\n455:         )\n456:     except OSError as exc:\n457:         raise PacketError(f\"cannot execute sprintctl reservation read: {exc}\") from exc\n458:     if completed.returncode != 0:\n459:         detail = completed.stderr.strip() or completed.stdout.strip()\n460:         raise PacketError(f\"served reservation read failed: {detail or completed.returncode}\")\n461:     try:\n462:         reservations = json.loads(completed.stdout)\n463:     except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:\n464:         raise PacketError(\"served reservation read returned invalid JSON\") from exc\n465:     if not isinstance(reservations, list):\n466:         raise PacketError(\"served reservation read returned a non-list payload\")\n467:     matching = [\n468:         row for row in reservations\n469:         if isinstance(row, dict) and row.get(\"id\") == expected_claim_id\n470:     ]\n471:     if len(matching) != 1:\n472:         raise PacketError(\n473:             f\"served reservation {expected_claim_id} is not active for item #{item_id}\"\n474:         )\n475:     reservation = matching[0]\n476:     if reservation.get(\"work_item_id\") != item_id:\n477:         raise PacketError(\n478:             f\"served reservation {expected_claim_id} does not cover item #{item_id}\"\n479:         )\n480:     if reservation.get(\"actor\") != expected_actor:\n481:         raise PacketError(\n482:             f\"served reservation {expected_claim_id} is not held by {expected_actor!r}\"\n483:         )\n484:     if reservation.get(\"state\") != \"active\":\n485:         raise PacketError(f\"served reservation {expected_claim_id} is not active\")\n486:     last_activity_at = reservation.get(\"last_activity_at\")\n487:     if not isinstance(last_activity_at, str):\n488:         raise PacketError(\n489:             f\"served reservation {expected_claim_id} has no last_activity_at\"\n490:         )\n491:     try:\n492:         touched = datetime.fromisoformat(last_activity_at.replace(\"Z\", \"+00:00\"))\n493:     except ValueError as exc:\n494:         raise PacketError(\n495:             f\"served reservation {expected_claim_id} has an invalid last_activity_at\"\n496:         ) from exc\n497:     if touched.tzinfo is None:\n498:         touched = touched.replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)\n499:     idle = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - touched\n500:     if idle > reservation_stale_after():\n501:         raise PacketError(\n502:             f\"served reservation {expected_claim_id} has been idle for \"\n503:             f\"{idle.total_seconds() / 3600:.1f}h; touch it deliberately \"\n504:             f\"(sprintctl reservation touch) or reserve the item again\"\n505:         )\n506:     return {\n507:         \"claim_id\": expected_claim_id,\n508:         \"work_item_id\": item_id,\n509:         \"agent\": expected_actor,\n510:         \"last_activity_at\": last_activity_at,\n511:         \"idle_seconds\": int(idle.total_seconds()),\n512:         \"checked_at\": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec=\"seconds\"),\n513:     }\n514: \n515: \n516: def build_overlay(\n517:     packet: dict[str, Any],\n518:     manifest: dict[str, Any],\n519:     policy: dict[str, Any],\n520:     base_config: dict[str, Any],\n521:     model_override: str | None = None,\n522: ) -> dict[str, Any]:\n523:     \"\"\"Build the session-only OpenCode permission overlay for one packet.\n524: \n525:     Noninteractive ``opencode run`` refuses permissions left at ``ask``, and\n526:     ``--auto`` is far too broad for a frozen packet, so every permission is\n527:     resolved here to an explicit allow or deny.\n528: \n529:     The qualified OpenCode implementation profile constrains the shape. Its\n530:     current devbox observation was measured against\n531:     ``opencode-go/deepseek-v4-flash`` on a trivial single-file edit:\n532: \n533:     * A wildcard ``\"*\": \"deny\"`` does not merely gate calls, it withholds the\n534:       tools from the model entirely. A blanket top-level deny left the worker\n535:       with no toolset at all, so it emitted a pseudo tool call as prose\n536:       (``<read_file src=.../>``) and stopped with an empty diff. Every tool is\n537:       therefore enumerated explicitly and the blanket deny is gone.\n538:     * The same applies inside a per-tool map: an ``edit`` map whose ``\"*\"`` is\n539:       ``deny`` withholds the edit tool even when specific paths are allowed, so\n540:       per-path scoping of ``edit`` silently guarantees an empty diff. ``bash``\n541:       does not share this behaviour and keeps its registered-command map.\n542: \n543:     So ``edit`` is granted whole and write containment rests where it is\n544:     actually adjudicated anyway: the disposable exact-commit worktree,\n545:     ``external_directory: deny``, and the cold ``diff-scope-respected`` /\n546:     ``protected-paths-untouched`` post-gates. The packet's\n547:     ``writable_patch_paths`` stay the contract the gates enforce; the overlay is\n548:     defence in depth over them, never the sole boundary.\n549:     \"\"\"\n550:     route = packet[\"route\"]\n551:     agent_name = policy[\"routes\"][route][\"agent\"]\n552:     base_agent = base_config[\"agent\"][agent_name]\n553:     commands = manifest[\"hybrid\"][\"commands\"]\n554: \n555:     # OpenCode evaluates matching permission patterns in insertion order and\n556:     # the last match wins.  Put the fallback first so an exact registered\n557:     # command can override it; appending \"*\" last withholds the bash tool even\n558:     # when an earlier command entry says allow.\n559:     bash: dict[str, str] = {\"*\": \"deny\"}\n560:     for command_id in packet[\"allowed_command_ids\"]:\n561:         bash[commands[command_id]] = \"allow\"\n562: \n563:     # Read-side tools the worker needs to locate its own work. Without these\n564:     # the model cannot inspect the tree it is asked to patch.\n565:     permission = {\n566:         \"read\": \"allow\",\n567:         \"glob\": \"allow\",\n568:         \"grep\": \"allow\",\n569:         \"list\": \"allow\",\n570:         \"todowrite\": \"allow\",\n571:         \"todoread\": \"allow\",\n572:         \"edit\": \"allow\",\n573:         \"write\": \"allow\",\n574:         \"patch\": \"allow\",\n575:         \"bash\": bash,\n576:         \"task\": \"deny\",\n577:         \"external_directory\": \"deny\",\n578:         \"webfetch\": \"deny\",\n579:         \"websearch\": \"deny\",\n580:     }\n581: \n582:     model = model_override or base_agent[\"model\"]\n583:     overlay = {\n584:         \"$schema\": base_config.get(\"$schema\", \"https://opencode.ai/config.json\"),\n585:         \"model\": model,\n586:         # The session overlay replaces the worker's config, so the route's\n587:         # declared provider registry has to travel or a model alias such as\n588:         # local3090/worker-fast cannot resolve. It travels narrowed: the\n589:         # credential is replaced with a placeholder and unknown keys are\n590:         # dropped. See worker_provider_registry.\n591:         \"provider\": worker_provider_registry(base_config),\n592:         \"permission\": dict(permission),\n593:         \"agent\": {\n594:             agent_name: {\n595:                 **base_agent,\n596:                 \"model\": model,\n597:                 \"permission\": {\n598:                     **base_agent.get(\"permission\", {}),\n599:                     **permission,\n600:                 },\n601:             }\n602:         },\n603:     }\n604:     if agent_name == \"ao-review\":\n605:         # The challenger reads a captured diff and never mutates the worktree,\n606:         # so every write surface is withheld, not just `edit`.\n607:         for surface in (\"edit\", \"write\", \"patch\", \"bash\"):\n608:             overlay[\"permission\"][surface] = \"deny\"\n609:             overlay[\"agent\"][agent_name][\"permission\"][surface] = \"deny\"\n610:     return overlay\n611: \n612: \n613: #: The sudo flags that make a worker contained, defined once so the\n614: #: qualification probe and production dispatch cannot drift apart. They did:\n615: #: --set-home was added to dispatch and not to the probe, so every profile\n616: #: probe measured the CLI under the coordinator's HOME while dispatch ran under\n617: #: the worker's, and the qualification evidence stopped describing production.\n618: CONTAINED_SUDO_FLAGS: tuple[str, ...] = (\"--non-interactive\", \"--set-home\")\n619: \n620: #: What the worker's provider registry is allowed to carry, per provider and\n621: #: inside ``options``. Deny-by-default: an unrecognised key is dropped, not\n622: #: passed on, so adding a secret to the coordinator's config cannot leak by\n623: #: omission.\n624: _PROVIDER_KEYS: tuple[str, ...] = (\"npm\", \"name\", \"models\")\n625: _PROVIDER_OPTION_KEYS: tuple[str, ...] = (\"baseURL\",)\n626: \n627: #: openai-compatible clients will not construct without some apiKey string.\n628: #: Forcing this one means a provider that needs a real credential fails loudly\n629: #: at the worker instead of quietly succeeding with the coordinator's.\n630: WORKER_PLACEHOLDER_API_KEY = \"local-only\"\n631: \n632: \n633: def worker_provider_registry(base_config: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n634:     \"\"\"The provider registry a worker needs, with no credential in it.\n635: \n636:     A model alias such as ``local3090/worker-fast`` cannot resolve unless the\n637:     overlay carries the provider that defines it: ``OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT``\n638:     replaces the worker's configuration rather than merging with it, and the\n639:     worker has no configuration of its own -- probed 2026-08-24, its home does\n640:     not exist. So the registry has to travel.\n641: \n642:     What must not travel is ``options.apiKey``. The same probe established that\n643:     the worker authenticates with no credential file at all, which makes this\n644:     the only credential-shaped value that ever crosses the boundary, and the\n645:     worker's own declared contract is \"no authority, credentials, network, or\n646:     oracle ownership\".\n647:     \"\"\"\n648:     registry: dict[str, Any] = {}\n649:     for name, provider in (base_config.get(\"provider\") or {}).items():\n650:         if not isinstance(provider, dict):\n651:             continue\n652:         kept = {k: provider[k] for k in _PROVIDER_KEYS if k in provider}\n653:         options = provider.get(\"options\")\n654:         if isinstance(options, dict):\n655:             kept_options = {\n656:                 k: options[k] for k in _PROVIDER_OPTION_KEYS if k in options\n657:             }\n658:             kept_options[\"apiKey\"] = WORKER_PLACEHOLDER_API_KEY\n659:             kept[\"options\"] = kept_options\n660:         registry[name] = kept\n661:     return registry\n662: \n663: \n664: def worker_argv(\n665:     opencode_bin: str,\n666:     agent_name: str,\n667:     message: str,\n668:     packet_path: Path,\n669:     worker_user: str | None,\n670: ) -> list[str]:\n671:     \"\"\"The exact command one contained worker loop runs.\n672: \n673:     Extracted so it can be asserted over without running anything. It points the worker's HOME at its own\n677:     rather than at one it cannot read.\n678:     \"\"\"\n679:     argv = [\n680:         opencode_bin,\n681:         \"run\",\n682:         message,\n683:         \"--agent\",\n684:         agent_name,\n685:         \"--file\",\n686:         str(packet_path),\n687:         \"--format\",\n688:         \"json\",\n689:     ]\n690:     if not worker_user:\n691:         return argv\n692:     return [\n693:         \"sudo\",\n694:         *CONTAINED_SUDO_FLAGS,\n695:         \"--user\",\n696:         worker_user,\n697:         \"--preserve-env=OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT\",\n698:         *argv,\n699:     ]\n700: \n701: \n702: def overlay_hash(overlay: dict[str, Any]) -> str:\n703:     # Permission maps are ordered policy: OpenCode applies the last matching\n704:     # rule.  Sorting keys would make behaviorally different overlays share a\n705:     # digest, so preserve the insertion order used for dispatch.\n706:     payload = json.dumps(overlay, separators=(\",\", \":\"))\n707:     return hashlib.sha256(payload.encode(\"utf-8\")).hexdigest()\n708: \n709: \n710: def context_churn_limits(packet: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n711:     \"\"\"Resolve additive v1 churn controls without breaking older packets.\"\"\"\n712:     return packet.get(\"context_churn\") or {\n713:         \"max_repeated_reads_per_path\": 4,\n714:         # These are the measured leak vector for the coordinator's path.\n748: AGENT_CONTEXT_FILES = (\"AGENTS.md\", \"CLAUDE.md\", \".agents/AGENTS.md\")\n749: \n750: \n751: def reroot_agent_context(workspace: Path, repo_root: Path) -> list[str]:\n752:     \"\"\"Repoint absolute coordinator paths in auto-loaded context at the clone.\n753: \n754:     This is the actual escape mechanism, and it is not a git one. A repository's\n755:     ``AGENTS.md`` documents commands with the absolute checkout path; OpenCode\n756:     loads it into the prompt unasked; a worker given a packet it must *locate*\n757:     work for then explores those absolute paths and writes to them. It never\n758:     consults git to do so.\n759: \n760:     Rerooting only works in a standalone clone. Attempted first on a linked\n761:     worktree it changed nothing, because OpenCode resolved the project root to\n762:     the coordinator's checkout and loaded *that* copy -- which is why clone\n763:     isolation and this rewrite are one fix rather than two, and why neither\n764:     alone was sufficient.\n765:     \"\"\"\n766:     needle = str(repo_root)\n767:     rewritten: list[str] = []\n768:     for relative in AGENT_CONTEXT_FILES:\n769:         path = workspace / relative\n770:         if not path.is_file():\n771:             continue\n772:         original = path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n773:         if needle not in original:\n774:             continue\n775:         path.write_text(original.replace(needle, str(workspace)), encoding=\"utf-8\")\n776:         rewritten.append(relative)\n777:     return rewritten\n778: \n779: \n780: def restore_agent_context(workspace: Path, repo_root: Path) -> None:\n781:     \"\"\"Undo :func:`reroot_agent_context` where the worker left the file alone.\n782: \n783:     The rewrite is coordinator scaffolding, not worker output, so it must not\n784:     reach the captured diff and trip ``diff-scope-respected``. A file whose only\n785:     difference from its committed blob is the substitution is restored; one the\n786:     worker genuinely edited is left as found, so a real out-of-scope edit still\n787:     fails its gate instead of being quietly reverted.\n788:     \"\"\"\n789:     for relative in AGENT_CONTEXT_FILES:\n790:         path = workspace / relative\n791:         if not path.is_file():\n792:             continue\n793:         current = path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n794:         if str(workspace) not in current:\n795:             continue\n796:         restored = current.replace(str(workspace), str(repo_root))\n797:         if restored == _git(workspace, \"show\", f\"HEAD:{relative}\"):\n798:             path.write_text(restored, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n799: \n800: \n801: def safety_ref(repo_root: Path, packet: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n802:     \"\"\"Pin the coordinator's pre-dispatch commit behind a named ref.\n803: \n804:     Recoverability must be a property of the tool, not of operator discipline:\n805:     the ref is created here so nobody has to remember to checkpoint, and the\n806:     receipt carries the one command that undoes the wave. Uncommitted\n807:     coordinator work is *not* covered by it -- git cannot restore what was never\n808:     committed -- so a dirty tree is reported alongside as what remains at risk.\n809:     \"\"\"\n810:     name = f\"safety/pre-dispatch-{packet['task_id']}\"\n811:     head = _git(repo_root, \"rev-parse\", \"HEAD\").strip()\n812:     existing = _git(repo_root, \"for-each-ref\", \"--format=%(refname:short)\", f\"refs/heads/{name}\")\n813:     if not existing.strip():\n814:         _git(repo_root, \"branch\", name, head)\n815:     dirty = [\n816:         line.strip()\n817:         for line in _git(repo_root, \"status\", \"--porcelain\").splitlines()\n818:         if line.strip()\n819:     ]\n820:     return {\n821:         \"ref\": name,\n822:         \"commit\": head,\n823:         \"restore_command\": f\"git -C {repo_root} reset --hard {name}\",\n824:         \"uncommitted_paths_at_risk\": dirty,\n825:     }\n826: \n827: \n828: def worker_shared_gid(worker_user: str | None) -> int | None:\n829:     \"\"\"The gid of the one group the coordinator and the contained worker share.\n830: \n831:     Setting the group-write bit is useless unless the group is one the worker is\n832:     actually in, and the clone lands owned by the coordinator's *primary* group,\n833:     which it is not. Measured on devbox 2026-08-23: workspace ``agent:agent``\n834:     mode 775, worker in ``agentworker`` and ``agentdispatch`` -- so every write\n835:     the worker attempted inside its own workspace failed ``PermissionDenied``\n836:     while the mode looked correct.\n837:     \"\"\"\n838:     if not worker_user:\n839:         return None\n840:     try:\n841:         worker = pwd.getpwnam(worker_user)\n842:     except KeyError:\n843:         return None\n844:     worker_gids = {worker.pw_gid}\n845:     mine = set(os.getgroups())\n846:     for group in grp.getgrall():\n847:         if worker_user in group.gr_mem:\n848:             worker_gids.add(group.gr_gid)\n849:     shared = sorted(worker_gids & mine)\n850:     return shared[0] if shared else None\n851: \n852: \n853: def share_workspace_with_group(workspace: Path, worker_user: str | None = None) -> None:\n854:     \"\"\"Make the freshly cloned workspace writable by its owning group.\n855: \n856:     The workspace root is setgid and group-shared precisely so the coordinator\n857:     and the worker can both write it, but setgid only propagates group\n858:     *ownership* -- the mode still comes from the creating process's umask. The\n859:     clone is made by the coordinator at its own umask, so without this every\n860:     file lands ``0644 coordinator:shared`` and the worker, which is in the\n861:     group but is not the owner, can write none of it.\n862: \n863:     That state is worth naming because it is silent and looks like something\n864:     else: measured on devbox 2026-07-28, a correctly moded workspace root still\n865:     left the contained worker unable to edit the clone it was dispatched into.\n866:     Its first edit fails with EACCES inside its *own* workspace, which reads\n867:     exactly like the containment boundary working -- and it is why the\n868:     containment probe alone (``test -w`` on the coordinator checkout) is not\n869:     sufficient evidence that a contained run can do anything.\n870: \n871:     Inert where no worker split exists: the workspace is then owned by the\n872:     coordinator's own group and nothing else is in it.\n873:     \"\"\"\n874:     gid = worker_shared_gid(worker_user)\n875:     for path in [workspace, *workspace.rglob(\"*\")]:\n876:         try:\n877:             mode = path.lstat().st_mode\n878:             if stat.S_ISLNK(mode):\n879:                 continue\n880:             # Group ownership first: the write bit means nothing on a group the\n881:             # worker is not in, which is the state that produced a silent\n882:             # 1.07M-token run where every edit was denied inside the worker's\n883:             # own workspace.\n884:             if gid is not None:\n885:                 os.chown(path, -1, gid)\n886:             path.chmod(stat.S_IMODE(mode) | stat.S_IWGRP)\n887:         except OSError:\n888:             # A single unreadable or racing path is not worth failing the whole\n889:             # prepare over; the worker's first write would surface it, and the\n890:             # group-write round trip is asserted by check-worker-containment.sh.\n891:             continue\n892: \n893: \n894: #: Every way the writability question can be answered. ``probed`` is reserved\n895: #: for the one case where the dynamic probe actually ran and succeeded; every\n896: #: other non-denial names why it did not run. A skipped check that reported the\n897: #: same as a passed one is what let RetryWorkspaceReuseTests stay green on a\n898: #: host where the probe never executed, so the distinction is the point.\n899: WRITABILITY_STATUSES = (\n900:     \"probed\",\n901:     \"denied\",\n902:     \"static-only\",\n903:     \"skipped:no-worker-user\",\n904:     \"skipped:probe-unavailable\",\n905:     \"skipped:probe-not-permitted\",\n906: )\n907: \n908: \n909: def _writability(writable: bool, status: str, detail: str) -> dict[str, Any]:\n910:     assert status in WRITABILITY_STATUSES, status\n911:     assert writable is (status != \"denied\"), (writable, status)\n912:     return {\"writable\": writable, \"status\": status, \"detail\": detail}\n913: \n914: \n915: def assess_worker_workspace_write(\n916:     workspace: Path, worker_user: str | None\n917: ) -> dict[str, Any]:\n918:     \"\"\"Prove the contained worker can actually write the workspace it is given.\n919: \n920:     This is the check whose absence cost a whole run. On 2026-08-23 a worker went\n921:     straight for the right three files, was denied on every one, spent 1.07M\n922:     tokens probing why, and hit its token ceiling -- while prepare reported a\n923:     green cold gate and run exited 0. A probe that cannot run is *skipped*, never treated as a\n933:     denial: refusing to dispatch because we lack sudo rights to ask would fail\n934:     the run for a reason that has nothing to do with _writability(False, \"denied\", f\"cannot stat {workspace}: {exc}\")\n949:     if gid is None:\n950:         return _writability(False, \"denied\", (\n951:             f\"{worker_user} shares no group \n1003: def workspace_is_retry_reuse(packet: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:\n1004:     \"\"\"True when an existing workspace is this packet's own, left by attempt N-1.\n1005: \n1006:     Narrow on purpose: the packet must *declare* a retry, and the standing\n1007:     workspace must be a git work tree already on the branch this packet names.\n1008:     Anything else -- a stale directory, someone else's branch, a first attempt --\n1009:     is still the two-workers case and is still refused.\n1010:     \"\"\"\n1011:     if int(packet.get(\"attempt\", 1) or 1) <= 1:\n1012:         return False\n1013:     target = worktree_path(packet)\n1014:     if not (target / \".git\").exists():\n1015:         return False\n1016:     try:\n1017:         branch = _git(target, \"rev-parse\", \"--abbrev-ref\", \"HEAD\").strip()\n1018:     except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - an unreadable work tree is not a reusable one\n1019:         return False\n1020:     return branch == packet[\"worktree\"][\"branch\"]\n1021: \n1022: \n1023: def prepare_workspace(\n1024:     repo_root: Path, packet: dict[str, Any], worker_user: str | None = None,\n1025: ) -> Path:\n1026:     \"\"\"Clone the repository into a disposable standalone workspace.\n1027: \n1028:     This provides **execution isolation and provenance, not containment.** It\n1029:     buys: no push route back to the coordinator (``origin`` is removed), an\n1030:     independent object store (``--no-hardlinks``), and disposable per-packet\n1031:     task state that can be destroyed without touching coordinator refs.\n1032: \n1033:     It does **not** stop a worker reaching the coordinator's checkout. That was\n1034:     the original justification and it was wrong: a bare probe inside such a\n1035:     clone stayed put, but the same clone driven through the full packet path\n1036:     still wrote to the coordinator's absolute paths on its first tool call.\n1037:     Rewriting those paths out of the auto-loaded ``AGENTS.md`` did not help\n1038:     either. The channel is neither git topology nor the context file, and it is\n1039:     deliberately left uninvestigated: once the filesystem denies the write, the\n1040:     source of the path stops mattering.\n1041: \n1042:     Filesystem containment is the worker uid (and later a mount namespace).\n1043:     See ``coordinator_tree_state`` for the detection that backs it up.\n1044:     \"\"\"\n1045:     target = worktree_path(packet)\n1046:     if target.exists():\n1047:         # The guard is about two workers at once. sprintctl_bin: str,\n) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Read and validate the exact served reservation that authorizes this packet.\n\n    The worker never receives this authority.  This coordinator-side check\n    prevents a frozen packet from being dispatched after its authorization went\n    stale, moved to a different actor, or ceased to cover the intended item.\n\n    **Migrated from ``sprintctl claim`` (2026-08-23).**  That command no longer\n    exists: sprintctl removed credential-bearing claims in favour of advisory\n    reservations, so every packet on every host failed here regardless of its\n    contents.  The unit tests did not catch it because they mocked the old\n    payload shape, which is the failure mode of testing a CLI you never call.\n\n    The two are not a rename.  A claim was a lease with an expiry; a reservation\n    is explicitly \"a coordination signal, not a lease\" and carries no expiry at\n    all.  The expiry check is therefore replaced by a **staleness** check on\n    ``last_activity_at``, matching sprintctl's own 4-hour horizon: an\n    authorization nobody has touched in that long is not evidence that anyone is\n    still coordinating this work, and ``sprintctl reservation touch`` refreshes\n    it deliberately rather than by accident.  ``sprint_item.claim_id`` and\n    ``claim_actor`` keep their names so frozen packets stay readable; they now\n    hold the reservation id and its actor.\n    \"\"\"\n    item_ref = packet[\"sprint_item\"][\"ref\"]\n    _, _, item_text = item_ref.partition(\"#\")\n    item_id = int(item_text)\n    expected_claim_id = packet[\"sprint_item\"][\"claim_id\"]\n    expected_actor = packet[\"sprint_item\"][\"claim_actor\"]\n    try:\n        completed = subprocess.run(\n            [sprintctl_bin, \"reservation\", \"list\", \"--item-id\", str(item_id),\n             \"--active-only\", \"--json\"],\n            cwd=repo_root,\n exc\n    if completed.returncode != 0:\n        detail = completed.stderr.strip() or completed.stdout.strip()\n        raise PacketError(f\"served reservation read failed: {detail or completed.returncode}\")\n    try:\n        reservations = json.loads(completed.stdout)\n    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:\n        raise PacketError(\"served reservation read returned invalid JSON\") from exc\n    if not isinstance(reservations, list):\n        raise PacketError(\"served reservation read returned a non-list payload\")\n    matching = [\n        row for row in reservations\n        if isinstance(row, dict) and row.get(\"id\") == expected_claim_id\n    ]\n    if len(matching) != 1:\n        raise PacketError(\n            f\"served reservation {expected_claim_id} is not active for item #{item_id}\"\n        )\n    reservation = matching[0]\n    if reservation.get(\"work_item_id\") != item_id:\n        raise PacketError(\n            f\"served reservation {expected_claim_id} does not cover item #{item_id}\"\n        )\n    if reservation.get(\"actor\") != expected_actor:\n        raise PacketError(\n            f\"served reservation {expected_claim_id} is not held by {expected_actor!r}\"\n        )\n    if reservation.get(\"state\") != \"active\":\n        raise PacketError(f\"served reservation {expected_claim_id} is not active\")\n    last_activity_at = reservation.get(\"last_activity_at\")\n    if not isinstance(last_activity_at, str):\n        raise PacketError(\n            f\"served reservation {expected_claim_id} has no last_activity_at\"\n        )\n    try:\n        touched = datetime.fromisoformat(last_activity_at.replace(\"Z\", \"+00:00\"))\n    except ValueError as exc:\n        raise PacketError(\n            f\"served reservation {expected_claim_id} has an invalid last_activity_at\"\n        ) from exc\n    if touched.tzinfo is None:\n        touched = touched.replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)\n    idle = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - touched\n    if idle > reservation_stale_after():\n        raise PacketError(\n            f\"served reservation {expected_claim_id} has been idle for \"\n            f\"{idle.total_seconds() / 3600:.1f}h; touch it deliberately \"\n            f\"(sprintctl reservation touch) or reserve the item again\"\n        )\n    return {\n        \"claim_id\": expected_claim_id,\n        \"work_item_id\": item_id,\n        \"agent\": expected_actor,\n        \"last_activity_at\": last_activity_at,\n        \"idle_seconds\": int(idle.total_seconds()),\n        \"checked_at\": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec=\"seconds\"),\n    }\n\n\ndef build_overlay(\n    packet: dict[str, Any],\n    manifest: dict[str, Any],\n    policy: dict[str, Any],\n    base_config: dict[str, Any],\n    model_override: str | None = None,\n) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Build the session-only OpenCode permission overlay for one packet.\n\n    Noninteractive ``opencode run`` refuses permissions left at ``ask``, and\n    ``--auto`` is far too broad for a frozen packet, so every permission is\n    resolved here to an explicit allow or deny.\n\n    The qualified OpenCode implementation profile constrains the shape. Its\n    current devbox observation was measured against\n    ``opencode-go/deepseek-v4-flash`` on a trivial single-file edit:\n\n    * A wildcard ``\"*\": \"deny\"`` does not merely gate calls, it withholds the\n      tools from the model entirely. A blanket top-level deny left the worker\n      with no toolset at all, so it emitted a pseudo tool call as prose\n      (``<read_file src=.../>``) and stopped with an empty diff. Every tool is\n      therefore enumerated explicitly and the blanket deny is gone.\n    * The same applies inside a per-tool map: an ``edit`` map whose ``\"*\"`` is\n      ``deny`` withholds the edit tool even when specific paths are allowed, so\n      per-path scoping of ``edit`` silently guarantees an empty diff. ``bash``\n      does not share this behaviour and keeps its registered-command map.\n\n    So ``edit`` is granted whole and write containment rests where it is\n    actually adjudicated anyway: the disposable exact-commit worktree,\n    ``external_directory: deny``, and the cold ``diff-scope-respected`` /\n    ``protected-paths-untouched`` post-gates. The packet's\n    ``writable_patch_paths`` stay the contract the gates enforce; the overlay is\n    defence in depth over them, never the sole boundary.\n    \"\"\"\n    route = packet[\"route\"]\n    agent_name = policy[\"routes\"][route][\"agent\"]\n    base_agent = base_config[\"agent\"][agent_name]\n    commands = manifest[\"hybrid\"][\"commands\"]\n\n    # OpenCode evaluates matching permission patterns in insertion order and\n    # the last match wins.  Put the fallback first so an exact registered\n    # command can override it; appending \"*\" last withholds the bash tool even\n    # when an earlier command entry says allow.\n    bash: dict[str, str] = {\"*\": \"deny\"}\n    for command_id in packet[\"allowed_command_ids\"]:\n        bash[commands[command_id]] = \"allow\"\n\n    # Read-side tools the worker needs to locate its own work. Without these\n    # the model cannot inspect the tree it is asked to patch.\n    permission = {\n        \"read\": \"allow\",\n        \"glob\": \"allow\",\n        \"grep\": \"allow\",\n        \"list\": \"allow\",\n        \"todowrite\": \"allow\",\n        \"todoread\": \"allow\",\n        \"edit\": \"allow\",\n        \"write\": \"allow\",\n        \"patch\": \"allow\",\n        \"bash\": bash,\n        \"task\": \"deny\",\n        \"external_directory\": \"deny\",\n        \"webfetch\": \"deny\",\n        \"websearch\": \"deny\",\n    }\n\n    model = model_override or base_agent[\"model\"]\n    overlay = {\n        \"$schema\": base_config.get(\"$schema\", \"https://opencode.ai/config.json\"),\n        \"model\": model,\n        # The session overlay replaces the worker's config, so the route's\n        # declared provider registry has to travel or a model alias such as\n        # local3090/worker-fast cannot resolve. It travels narrowed: the\n        # credential is replaced with a placeholder and unknown keys are\n        # dropped. See worker_provider_registry.\n        \"provider\": worker_provider_registry(base_config),\n        \"permission\": dict(permission),\n        \"agent\": {\n            agent_name: {\n                **base_agent,\n                \"model\": model,\n                \"permission\": {\n                    **base_agent.get(\"permission\", {}),\n                    **permission,\n                },\n            }\n        },\n    }\n    if agent_name == \"ao-review\":\n        # The challenger reads a captured diff and never mutates the worktree,\n        # so every write surface is withheld, not just `edit`.\n        for surface in (\"edit\", \"write\", \"patch\", \"bash\"):\n            overlay[\"permission\"][surface] = \"deny\"\n            overlay[\"agent\"][agent_name][\"permission\"][surface] = \"deny\"\n    return overlay\n\n\n#: The sudo flags that make a worker contained, defined once so the\n#: qualification probe and production dispatch cannot drift apart. They did:\n#: --set-home was added to dispatch and not to the probe, so every profile\n#: probe measured the CLI under the coordinator's HOME while dispatch ran under\n#: the worker's, and the qualification evidence stopped describing production.\nCONTAINED_SUDO_FLAGS: tuple[str, ...] = (\"--non-interactive\", \"--set-home\")\n\n#: What the worker's provider registry is allowed to carry, per provider and\n#: inside ``options``. Deny-by-default: an unrecognised key is dropped, not\n#: passed on, so adding a secret to the coordinator's config cannot leak by\n#: omission.\n_PROVIDER_KEYS: tuple[str, ...] = (\"npm\", \"name\", \"models\")\n_PROVIDER_OPTION_KEYS: tuple[str, ...] = (\"baseURL\",)\n\n#: openai-compatible clients will not construct without some apiKey string.\n#: Forcing this one means a provider that needs a real credential fails loudly\n#: at the worker instead of quietly succeeding with the coordinator's.\nWORKER_PLACEHOLDER_API_KEY = \"local-only\"\n\n\ndef worker_provider_registry(base_config: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"The provider registry a worker needs, with no credential in it.\n\n    A model alias such as ``local3090/worker-fast`` cannot resolve unless the\n    overlay carries the provider that defines it: ``OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT``\n    replaces the worker's configuration rather than merging with it, and the\n    worker has no configuration of its own -- probed 2026-08-24, its home does\n    not exist. So the registry has to travel.\n\n    What must not travel is ``options.apiKey``. The same probe established that\n    the worker authenticates with no credential file at all, which makes this\n    the only credential-shaped value that ever crosses the boundary, and the\n    worker's own declared contract is \"no authority, credentials, network, or\n    oracle ownership\".\n    \"\"\"\n    registry: dict[str, Any] = {}\n    for name, provider in (base_config.get(\"provider\") or {}).items():\n        if not isinstance(provider, dict):\n            continue\n        kept = {k: provider[k] for k in _PROVIDER_KEYS if k in provider}\n        options = provider.get(\"options\")\n        if isinstance(options, dict):\n            kept_options = {\n                k: options[k] for k in _PROVIDER_OPTION_KEYS if k in options\n            }\n            kept_options[\"apiKey\"] = WORKER_PLACEHOLDER_API_KEY\n            kept[\"options\"] = kept_options\n        registry[name] = kept\n    return registry\n\n\ndef worker_argv(\n    opencode_bin: str,\n    agent_name: str,\n    message: str,\n    packet_path: Path,\n    worker_user: str | None,\n) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"The exact command one contained worker loop runs.\n\n    Extracted so it can be asserted over without running anything. Note what\n    ``--set-home`` does and does not do: the coordinator's opencode auth store\n    and ssh directory are already unreadable by the worker on filesystem\n    permissions, so this closes no leak. It points the worker's HOME at its own\n    rather than at one it cannot read.\n    \"\"\"\n    argv = [\n        opencode_bin,\n        \"run\",\n        message,\n        \"--agent\",\n        agent_name,\n        \"--file\",\n        str(packet_path),\n        \"--format\",\n        \"json\",\n    ]\n    if not worker_user:\n        return argv\n    return [\n        \"sudo\",\n        *CONTAINED_SUDO_FLAGS,\n        \"--user\",\n        worker_user,\n        \"--preserve-env=OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT\",\n        *argv,\n    ]\n\n\ndef overlay_hash(overlay: dict[str, Any]) -> str:\n    # Permission maps are ordered policy: OpenCode applies the last matching\n    # rule.  Sorting keys would make behaviorally different overlays share a\n    # digest, so preserve the insertion order used for dispatch.\n    payload = json.dumps(overlay, separators=(\",\", \":\"))\n    return hashlib.sha256(payload.encode(\"utf-8\")).hexdigest()\n\n\ndef context_churn_limits(packet: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Resolve additive v1 churn controls without breaking older packets.\"\"\"\n    return packet.get(\"context_churn\") or {\n        \"max_repeated_reads_per_path\": 4,\n        # Every workspace the loop builds is a full clone of the repository, and\n        # eight steps is not enough room to orient in one before writing.\n        \"max_reasoning_steps_without_mutation\": 12,\n        \"max_identical_context_tokens\": 250000,\n        \"handoff_when_candidate_ready\": True,\n    }\n\n\ndef gate_set_hash_schema_version(packet: dict[str, Any]) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return the version needed to compare this receipt's gate-set hash.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        return GATE_SET_HASH_SCHEMA_VERSIONS[packet[\"schema_version\"]]\n    except KeyError as exc:\n        raise PacketError(f\"unsupported packet schema_version {packet.get('schema_version')!r}\") from exc\n\n\ndef _git(repo_root: Path, *args: str) -> str:\n    result = subprocess.run(\n        [\"git\", \"-C\", str(repo_root), *args],\n        capture_output=True,\n        text=True,\n        check=False,\n    )\n    if result.returncode != 0:\n        raise PacketError(f\"git {' '.join(args)} failed: {result.stderr.strip()}\")\n    return result.stdout\n\n\ndef worktree_path(packet: dict[str, Any]) -> Path:\n    return Path(packet[\"worktree\"][\"root\"]) / packet[\"repo_id\"] / packet[\"task_id\"]\n\n\n#: Agent-context files OpenCode auto-loads into the worker's prompt without a\n#: tool call. These are the measured leak vector for the coordinator's path.\nAGENT_CONTEXT_FILES = (\"AGENTS.md\", \"CLAUDE.md\", \".agents/AGENTS.md\")\n\n\ndef reroot_agent_context(workspace: Path, repo_root: Path) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Repoint absolute coordinator paths in auto-loaded context at the clone.\n\n    This is the actual escape mechanism, and it is not a git one. A repository's\n    ``AGENTS.md`` documents commands with the absolute checkout path; OpenCode\n    loads it into the prompt unasked; a worker given a packet it must *locate*\n    work for then explores those absolute paths and writes to them. It never\n    consults git to do so.\n\n    Rerooting only works in a standalone clone. Attempted first on a linked\n    worktree it changed nothing, because OpenCode resolved the project root to\n    the coordinator's checkout and loaded *that* copy -- which is why clone\n    isolation and this rewrite are one fix rather than two, and why neither\n    alone was sufficient.\n    \"\"\"\n    needle = str(repo_root)\n    rewritten: list[str] = []\n    for relative in AGENT_CONTEXT_FILES:\n        path = workspace / relative\n        if not path.is_file():\n            continue\n        original = path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        if needle not in original:\n            continue\n        path.write_text(original.replace(needle, str(workspace)), encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        rewritten.append(relative)\n    return rewritten\n\n\ndef restore_agent_context(workspace: Path, repo_root: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Undo :func:`reroot_agent_context` where the worker left the file alone.\n\n    The rewrite is coordinator scaffolding, not worker output, so it must not\n    reach the captured diff and trip ``diff-scope-respected``. A file whose only\n    difference from its committed blob is the substitution is restored; one the\n    worker genuinely edited is left as found, so a real out-of-scope edit still\n    fails its gate instead of being quietly reverted.\n    \"\"\"\n    for relative in AGENT_CONTEXT_FILES:\n        path = workspace / relative\n        if not path.is_file():\n            continue\n        current = path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        if str(workspace) not in current:\n            continue\n        restored = current.replace(str(workspace), str(repo_root))\n        if restored == _git(workspace, \"show\", f\"HEAD:{relative}\"):\n            path.write_text(restored, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n\n\ndef safety_ref(repo_root: Path, packet: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Pin the coordinator's pre-dispatch commit behind a named ref.\n\n    Recoverability must be a property of the tool, not of operator discipline:\n    the ref is created here so nobody has to remember to checkpoint, and the\n    receipt carries the one command that undoes the wave. Uncommitted\n    coordinator work is *not* covered by it -- git cannot restore what was never\n    committed -- so a dirty tree is reported alongside as what remains at risk.\n    \"\"\"\n    name = f\"safety/pre-dispatch-{packet['task_id']}\"\n    head = _git(repo_root, \"rev-parse\", \"HEAD\").strip()\n    existing = _git(repo_root, \"for-each-ref\", \"--format=%(refname:short)\", f\"refs/heads/{name}\")\n    if not existing.strip():\n        _git(repo_root, \"branch\", name, head)\n    dirty = [\n        line.strip()\n        for line in _git(repo_root, \"status\", \"--porcelain\").splitlines()\n        if line.strip()\n    ]\n    return {\n        \"ref\": name,\n        \"commit\": head,\n        \"restore_command\": f\"git -C {repo_root} reset --hard {name}\",\n        \"uncommitted_paths_at_risk\": dirty,\n    }\n\n\ndef worker_shared_gid(worker_user: str | None) -> int | None:\n    \"\"\"The gid of the one group the coordinator and the contained worker share.\n\n    Setting the group-write bit is useless unless the group is one the worker is\n    actually in, and the clone lands owned by the coordinator's *primary* group,\n    which it is not. Measured on devbox 2026-08-23: workspace ``agent:agent``\n    mode 775, worker in ``agentworker`` and ``agentdispatch`` -- so every write\n    the worker attempted inside its own workspace failed ``PermissionDenied``\n    while the mode looked correct.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not worker_user:\n        return None\n    try:\n        worker = pwd.getpwnam(worker_user)\n    except KeyError:\n        return None\n    worker_gids = {worker.pw_gid}\n    mine = set(os.getgroups())\n    for group in grp.getgrall():\n        if worker_user in group.gr_mem:\n            worker_gids.add(group.gr_gid)\n    shared = sorted(worker_gids & mine)\n    return shared[0] if shared else None\n\n\ndef share_workspace_with_group(workspace: Path, worker_user: str | None = None) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Make the freshly cloned workspace writable by its owning group.\n\n    The workspace root is setgid and group-shared precisely so the coordinator\n    and the worker can both write it, but setgid only propagates group\n    *ownership* -- the mode still comes from the creating process's umask. The\n    clone is made by the coordinator at its own umask, so without this every\n    file lands ``0644 coordinator:shared`` and the worker, which is in the\n    group but is not the owner, can write none of it.\n\n    That state is worth naming because it is silent and looks like something\n    else: measured on devbox 2026-07-28, a correctly moded workspace root still\n    left the contained worker unable to edit the clone it was dispatched into.\n    Its first edit fails with EACCES inside its *own* workspace, which reads\n    exactly like the containment boundary working -- and it is why the\n    containment probe alone (``test -w`` on the coordinator checkout) is not\n    sufficient evidence that a contained run can do anything.\n\n    Inert where no worker split exists: the workspace is then owned by the\n    coordinator's own group and nothing else is in it.\n    \"\"\"\n    gid = worker_shared_gid(worker_user)\n    for path in [workspace, *workspace.rglob(\"*\")]:\n        try:\n            mode = path.lstat().st_mode\n            if stat.S_ISLNK(mode):\n                continue\n            # Group ownership first: the write bit means nothing on a group the\n            # worker is not in, which is the state that produced a silent\n            # 1.07M-token run where every edit was denied inside the worker's\n            # own workspace.\n            if gid is not None:\n                os.chown(path, -1, gid)\n            path.chmod(stat.S_IMODE(mode) | stat.S_IWGRP)\n        except OSError:\n            # A single unreadable or racing path is not worth failing the whole\n            # prepare over; the worker's first write would surface it, and the\n            # group-write round trip is asserted by check-worker-containment.sh.\n            continue\n\n\n#: Every way the writability question can be answered. ``probed`` is reserved\n#: for the one case where the dynamic probe actually ran and succeeded; every\n#: other non-denial names why it did not run. A skipped check that reported the\n#: same as a passed one is what let RetryWorkspaceReuseTests stay green on a\n#: host where the probe never executed, so the distinction is the point.\nWRITABILITY_STATUSES = (\n    \"probed\",\n    \"denied\",\n    \"static-only\",\n    \"skipped:no-worker-user\",\n    \"skipped:probe-unavailable\",\n    \"skipped:probe-not-permitted\",\n)\n\n\ndef _writability(writable: bool, status: str, detail: str) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    assert status in WRITABILITY_STATUSES, status\n    assert writable is (status != \"denied\"), (writable, status)\n    return {\"writable\": writable, \"status\": status, \"detail\": detail}\n\n\ndef assess_worker_workspace_write(\n    workspace: Path, worker_user: str | None\n) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Prove the contained worker can actually write the workspace it is given.\n\n    This is the check whose absence cost a whole run. On 2026-08-23 a worker went\n    straight for the right three files, was denied on every one, spent 1.07M\n    tokens probing why, and hit its token ceiling -- while prepare reported a\n    green cold gate and run exited 0. Nothing in the receipt said \"the worker\n    could not write\", because nothing had asked.\n\n    The decisive check is static and needs no privileges: the workspace's group\n    must be one the worker belongs to, and the group-write bit must be set.\n    That is exactly the state that failed, and it is knowable by stat alone.\n\n    The dynamic probe is a confirmation, not the check, because the coordinator\n    may run only a narrow allowlist as the worker -- on devbox that is opencode\n    and ``test``. A probe that cannot run is *skipped*, never treated as a\n    denial: refusing to dispatch because we lack sudo rights to ask would fail\n    the run for a reason that has nothing to do with the workspace.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not worker_user:\n        return _writability(\n            True, \"skipped:no-worker-user\",\n            \"no worker user: writes happen as the coordinator. --worker-user \"\n            \"defaults to $AGENTOPS_WORKER_USER, so this is what an unset \"\n            \"variable looks like, not a workspace that was checked\",\n        )\n\n    gid = worker_shared_gid(worker_user)\n    try:\n        info = workspace.lstat()\n    except OSError as exc:\n        return _writability(False, \"denied\", f\"cannot stat {workspace}: {exc}\")\n    if gid is None:\n        return _writability(False, \"denied\", (\n            f\"{worker_user} shares no group with this process, so no mode can make \"\n            f\"{workspace} writable by it\"\n        ))\n    if info.st_gid != gid:\n        return _writability(False, \"denied\", (\n            f\"{workspace} is group {info.st_gid}, not {gid}, which is the group \"\n            f\"{worker_user} is in: the group-write bit would be inert\"\n        ))\n    if not stat.S_IMODE(info.st_mode) & stat.S_IWGRP:\n        return _writability(False, \"denied\",\n            f\"{workspace} is not group-writable, so {worker_user} cannot write it\")\n\n    test_bin = shutil.which(\"test\", path=os.defpath) or shutil.which(\"test\")\n    if test_bin is None:\n        return _writability(True, \"skipped:probe-unavailable\",\n            f\"{workspace} is group-writable by {worker_user} (no test binary)\")\n    try:\n        completed = subprocess.run(\n            [\"sudo\", \"--non-interactive\", \"--user\", worker_user, test_bin, \"-w\", str(workspace)],\n            capture_output=True, text=True, check=False, timeout=30,\n        )\n    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired) as exc:\n        return _writability(True, \"skipped:probe-unavailable\",\n            f\"group ownership correct; probe could not run ({exc})\")\n    stderr = (completed.stderr or \"\").strip()\n    if completed.returncode != 0 and _probe_was_refused(stderr):\n        return _writability(True, \"skipped:probe-not-permitted\",\n            f\"group ownership correct; probe not permitted ({stderr[-160:]})\")\n    if completed.returncode != 0:\n        return _writability(False, \"denied\", (\n            f\"{worker_user} cannot write {workspace} despite correct group and mode: \"\n            f\"{stderr[-300:] or completed.returncode}\"\n        ))\n    return _writability(True, \"probed\",\n        f\"{worker_user} can write {workspace} (probe ran and succeeded)\")\n\n\ndef worker_can_write_workspace(workspace: Path, worker_user: str | None) -> tuple[bool, str]:\n    \"\"\"The two-value form, kept for callers that only need the verdict.\"\"\"\n    assessment = assess_worker_workspace_write(workspace, worker_user)\n    return assessment[\"writable\"], assessment[\"detail\"]\n\n\ndef _probe_was_refused(stderr: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Tell \"sudo would not let me ask\" apart from \"the answer is no\".\"\"\"\n    lowered = stderr.lower()\n    return any(\n        marker in lowered\n        for marker in (\"a password is required\", \"not allowed to execute\", \"may not run\")\n    )\n\n\ndef workspace_is_retry_reuse(packet: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"True when an existing workspace is this packet's own, left by attempt N-1.\n\n    Narrow on purpose: the packet must *declare* a retry, and the standing\n    workspace must be a git work tree already on the branch this packet names.\n    Anything else -- a stale \n1518: \n1519: def assess_cold_run(\n1520:     results: list[dict[str, Any]], packet: dict[str, Any]\n1521: ) -> tuple[list[dict[str, Any]], bool, list[str]]:\n1522:     \"\"\"Decide whether a cold run means the workspace is fit to dispatch into.\n1523: \n1524:     \"Every registered command is green\" is the right rule for a packet whose\n1525:     gates already pass, and the wrong one for a packet whose oracle is a test\n1526:     written before the code -- which is what a coordinator-authored oracle\n1527:     usually is.  Such a packet was undispatchable by construction: prepare\n1528:     demanded green, and the point of the packet was that one command is red.\n1529: \n1530:     ``oracle.starts_red`` resolves it *upward* rather than by exempting anything.\n1531:     A named command must be red here, and a command that is already green is a\n1532:     fault: an oracle that cannot fail at the starting commit is not evidence of\n1533:     anything the worker later does to it.  Everything else must still be green,\n1534:     so unrelated breakage is caught exactly as before.\n1535:     \"\"\"\n1536:     expected_red = set((packet.get(\"oracle\") or {}).get(\"starts_red\") or [])\n1537:     faults: list[str] = []\n1538:     for result in results:\n1539:         command_id = result[\"command_id\"]\n1540:         red = result[\"exit_code\"] != 0\n1541:         # 127 is \"command not found\", which is not a gate failing -- it is a gate\n1542:         # that does not exist at this commit. A\n1624:     worker that read 1.07M tokens without a single completed write ran to its\n1625:     token ceiling with the limit sitting unread in its own packet.\n1626: \n1627:     The stream is what the harness can see, so the limits are counted in it:\n1628:     tool events since the last *completed* mutation, and reads of one path. A\n1629:     denied write does not reset the counter -- it is an attempt, not progress --\n1630:     and a run of them gets its own verdict, because \"the worker cannot write\n1631:     here\" is a different fact from \"the worker is going in circles\" and the\n1632:     operator needs to be told which.\n1633:     \"\"\"\n1634:     max_steps = limits.get(\"max_reasoning_steps_without_mutation\")\n1635:     max_reads = limits.get(\"max_repeated_reads_per_path\")\n1636:     steps_since_mutation = 0\n1637:     failed_mutations = 0\n1638:     reads: dict[str, int] = {}\n1639:     for event in events:\n1640:         if event.get(\"type\") != \"tool_use\":\n1641:             continue\n1642:         part = event.get(\"part\") or {}\n1643:         tool = part.get(\"tool\")\n1644:         state = part.get(\"state\") or {}\n1645:         status = state.get(\"status\")\n1646:         if tool in MUTATION_TOOLS:\n1647:             if status == \"completed\":\n1648:                 steps_since_mutation = 0\n1649:                 failed_mutations = 0\n1650:                 continue\n1651:             failed_mutations += 1\n1652:             if failed_mutations >= 3:\n1653:                 return (\n1654:                     \"worker_cannot_write\",\n1655:                     f\"{failed_mutations} consecutive mutation attempts failed; the worker \"\n1656:                     \"is being denied inside its own workspace\",\n1657:                 )\n1658:         # A call that did not complete is the harness failing, not the worker\n1659:         # circling, and it must not spend the budget meant to catch circling.\n1660:         # V5-M9 died on this three times: the worker's bash was refused by its\n1661:         # own profile and a grep errored on ripgrep's record limit, and those\n1662:         # four failures exhausted the steps before a write was ever attempted.\n1663:         # A failed *mutation* is still counted above, because \"the worker is\n1664:         # being denied inside its own workspace\" is a different fact and the\n1665:         # operator needs to be told which.\n1666:         if status != \"completed\":\n1667:             continue\n1668:         steps_since_mutation += 1\n1669:         if max_steps and steps_since_mutation > max_steps:\n1670:             return (\n1671:                 \"churn_no_mutation\",\n1672:                 f\"{steps_since_mutation} tool steps without a completed mutation \"\n1673:                 f\"(limit {max_steps})\",\n1674:             )\n1675:         if tool == \"read\" and max_reads:\n1676:             path = (state.get(\"input\") or {}).get(\"filePath\")\n1677:             if path:\n1678:                 reads[path] = reads.get(path, 0) + 1\n1679:                 if reads[path] > max_reads:\n1680:                     return (\n1681:                         \"churn_repeated_reads\",\n1682:                         f\"{path} read {reads[path]} times (limit {max_reads})\",\n1683:                     )\n1684:     return None\n1685: \n1686: \n1687: def churn_metrics_for(events: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n1688:     \"\"\"Summarise ``events`` with the sibling ``churn_metrics`` module.\n1689: \n1690:     The import is deferred rather than made at module scope because\n1691:     ``churn_metrics.py`` loads *this* file by path to import ``MUTATION_TOOLS``\n1692:     rather than restating it. Importing it from here at import time would\n1693:     re-enter this module before it finished executing; at call time both are\n1694:     complete. None of it is a seam the packet could\n#: have declared, so flagging it would only teach packets to declare\n#: ``pyproject.toml`` as context. The oracle's own test files -- the ones the\n#: command names on its command line -- are exempted separately in\n#: ``_oracle_named_paths``: that a test reads itself is not a dependency.\nORACLE_READ_TRACE_EXEMPT_PREFIXES = (\".git/\", \"__pycache__/\", \".venv/\")\nORACLE_READ_TRACE_EXEMPT_FILES = (\n    \"pyproject.toml\", \"setup.cfg\", \"pytest.ini\", \"tox.ini\", \"conftest.py\",\n)\n#: strace's own file-syscall class. A hand-maintained list silently misses\n#: whatever a platform names differently; the class is what strace keeps current.\nORACLE_READ_TRACE_SYSCALLS = \"%file\"\n_STRACE_OPEN_RE = re.compile(\n    r'^(?:\\[pid\\s+\\d+\\]\\s*|\\d+\\s+)?(openat|open)\\((?:([^,]*),\\s*)?\"([^\"]*)\",\\s*([A-Z_|0-9]+)'\n    r'[^)]*\\)\\s*=\\s*(-?\\d+)'\n)\n\n\ndef _oracle_named_paths(checkout: Path, command: str) -> set[Path]:\n    \"\"\"Files or directories the command names on its own command line.\"\"\"\n    named: set[Path] = set()\n    try:\n        tokens = shlex.split(command)\n    except ValueError:\n        tokens = command.split()\n    for token in tokens:\n        # ``tests/test_x.py::TestCase::test_y`` names a file; strip the selector.\n        candidate = (checkout / token.split(\"::\", 1)[0])\n        if candidate.exists():\n            named.add(Path(os.path.realpath(candidate)))\n    return named\n\n\ndef _is_exempt_read(rel: str, resolved: Path, named: set[Path]) -> bool:\n    if rel in ORACLE_READ_TRACE_EXEMPT_FILES or rel.startswith(ORACLE_READ_TRACE_EXEMPT_PREFIXES):\n        return True\n    if \"__pycache__\" in Path(rel).parts:\n        return True\n    return any(resolved == n or n in resolved.parents for n in named)\n\n\ndef parse_strace_reads(trace_text: str, checkout: Path) -> set[str]:\n    \"\"\"Paths inside ``checkout`` that the traced command opened for reading.\n\n    Only *successful* opens that are not write-only count: a failed ``openat``\n    is a probe, not a read, and ``O_WRONLY`` is the oracle writing its own\n    cache. Directories are dropped -- a runner walks the tree to find tests,\n    and that walk is not a dependency on anything in it.\n    \"\"\"\n    root = Path(os.path.realpath(checkout))\n    reads: set[str] = set()\n    for line in trace_text.splitlines():\n        match = _STRACE_OPEN_RE.match(line)\n        if match is None:\n            continue\n        syscall, dirfd, raw_path, flags, result = match.groups()\n        if int(result) < 0 or \"O_WRONLY\" in flags.split(\"|\"):\n            continue\n        # ``openat``'s first argument is a directory fd, and a relative path is\n        # relative to *that*, not to the cwd. shutil.rmtree walks with such fds,\n        # so tempfile.TemporaryDirectory cleanup emits\n        # ``openat(3, \"coordinator\", ...)`` -- and resolving it against the\n        # checkout invented a read of a path that does not exist there. Every\n        # Python oracle in this repository uses TemporaryDirectory, so this\n        # false-positived all of them and made oracle_reads_within_paths\n        # unobtainable. AT_FDCWD is the one fd that does mean the cwd.\n        if (\n            not raw_path.startswith(\"/\")\n            and syscall == \"openat\"\n            and (dirfd or \"\").strip() != \"AT_FDCWD\"\n        ):\n            continue\n        resolved = Path(os.path.realpath(root / raw_path if not raw_path.startswith(\"/\") else raw_path))\n        if resolved == root or root not in resolved.parents or resolved.is_dir():\n            continue\n        reads.add(resolved.relative_to(root).as_posix())\n    return reads\n\n\ndef trace_oracle_reads(\n    checkout: Path,\n    packet: dict[str, Any],\n    commands: dict[str, str],\n    strace_bin: str | None,\n    allow_untraced: bool = False,\n) -> tuple[list[str], dict[str, Any]]:\n    \"\"\"L-2(b), read-trace half: every file a ``starts_red`` oracle reads at\n    ``starting_commit`` must be inside the paths the packet declares.\n\n    An oracle that reads a file the worker may neither see nor write is an\n    oracle that demands an unstated seam, or covers work the packet does not\n    contain -- two of the five defects behind the seven-run packet. The packet\n    cannot be judged by a test whose inputs it cannot name.\n\n    Fails closed without ``strace``: a check that silently did not run is\n    indistinguishable from one that passed. ``allow_untraced`` records the gap\n    on the report as ``\"skipped:untraced\"`` and never as ``True``.\n    \"\"\"\n    starts_red = (packet.get(\"oracle\") or {}).get(\"starts_red\") or []\n packet.get(\"writable_patch_paths\") or []\n    )\n    faults: list[str] = []\n    outside: dict[str, list[str]] = {}\n    for command_id in starts_red:\n        command = commands.get(command_id)\n commands: dict[str, str],\n    patch_path: Path | None,\n) -> tuple[list[str], dict[str, Any]]:\n    \"\"\"L-2(b), reference-overlay half: a change confined to\n    ``writable_patch_paths`` must turn every ``starts_red`` oracle green.\n\n    The read-trace half proves the oracle reads nothing undeclared; it cannot\n    prove the oracle is *satisfiable* by the files the worker may write. An\n    oracle that demands a seam outside ``writable_patch_paths`` (defect 3) or\n    that covers more work than the packet holds (defect 4) passes the trace and\n    still cannot be met. Applying a coordinator-supplied reference patch at\n    ``starting_commit`` and requiring green closes that gap at freeze time.\n\n    ``patch_path`` is the already-resolved location of the unified diff (it\n    lives in the coordinator's tree, not at ``starting_commit``). Absent, the\n    check is recorded as ``\"skipped:no-reference\"`` -- never ``True``.\n    \"\"\"\n    starts_red = (packet.get(\"oracle\") or {}).get(\"starts_red\") or []\n    report: dict[str, Any] patch_path is None:\n        report[\"oracle_satisfiable_within_paths\"] = \"skipped:no-reference\"\n        return [], report\n    report[\"reference_patch\"] = (packet.get(\"oracle\") or not a file\"], report\n    faults: list[str] = []\n    check = subprocess.run(\n        [\"git\", \"apply\", \"--check\", str(patch_path)], cwd=checkout,\n        stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL, capture_output=True, text=True, check=False,\n    )\n    if check.returncode != 0:\n        report[\"oracle_satisfiable_within_paths\"] = False\n        return [\n            f\"reference_patch does not apply at {packet['starting_commit'][:12]}: \"\n            f\"{check.stderr.strip()[-400:]}\"\n        ], report\n    numstat = subprocess.run(\n        [\"git\", \"apply\", \"--numstat\", str(patch_path)], cwd=checkout,\n        stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL, capture_output=True, text=True, check=False,\n    )\n    touched = sorted({\n        line.split(\"\\t\", 2)[2].strip()\n        for line in numstat.stdout.splitlines() if line.count(\"\\t\") >= 2\n    })\n    writable = list(packet.get(\"writable_patch_paths\") or [])\n    outside = [rel for rel in touched if not _matches_any(rel, writable)]\n    if outside:\n        report[\"oracle_satisfiable_within_paths\"] = False\n        return [\n            \"reference_patch touches files outside writable_patch_paths: \"\n            + \", \".join(outside)\n            + \" -- the oracle can only be met by writing where the worker may not\"\n        ], report\n    applied = subprocess.run(\n        [\"git\", \"apply\", str(patch_path)], cwd=checkout,\n        stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL, capture_output=True, text=True, check=False,\n    )\n    if applied.returncode != 0:\n        report[\"oracle_satisfiable_within_paths\"] = False\n        return [f\"reference_patch failed to apply: {applied.stderr.strip()[-400:]}\"], report\n    still_red: list[str] = []\n    for command_id in starts_red:\n        command = commands.get(command_id)\n        if command is None:\n            continue  # already a fault from check_oracle_attainable\n        completed = subprocess.run(\n            command, shell=True, cwd=checkout, stdin=subprocess.DEVNULL,\n            capture_output=True, text=True, check=False,\n            timeout=packet[\"limits\"][\"timeout_seconds\"],\n        )\n        if completed.returncode != 0:\n            still_red.append(command_id)\n            faults.append(\n                f\"{command_id} is still red (exit {completed.returncode}) after the reference \"\n                f\"patch at {packet['starting_commit'][:12]}: the oracle demands something \"\n                \"outside writable_patch_paths, or more than this packet holds\"\n            )\n    report[\"oracle_satisfiable_within_paths\"] = not still_red\n    report[\"red_after_reference\"] = still_red\n    return faults, report\n\n\ndef resolve_reference_patch(repo_root: Path, packet: dict[str, Any]) -> Path | None:\n    \"\"\"Where the packet's reference patch lives, or None when it carries none.\"\"\"\n    rel = (packet.get(\"oracle\") or {}).get(\"reference_patch\")\n    if not rel:\n        return None\n    if Path(rel).is_absolute() or _pattern_escapes_repo(rel):\n        raise PacketError(\"oracle.reference_patch must be a repo-relative path that stays inside the repository\")\n    return repo_root / rel\n\n\ndef check_oracle_reference(\n    repo_root: Path, packet: dict[str, Any], manifest: dict[str, Any],\n) -> tuple[list[str], dict[str, Any]]:\n    \"\"\"``overlay_reference_patch`` against a throwaway checkout of ``starting_commit``.\"\"\"\n    starts_red = (packet.get(\"oracle\") or {}).get(\"starts_red\") or []\n    empty = {\"oracle_satisfiable_within_paths\": None, \"reference_patch\": None, \"red_after_reference\": []}\n    if not starts_red:\n        return [], empty\n    patch_path =
